DMA bounds equivalence and current transport/integration evidence - 2026-10-31

Requirements: WSE-STD-BRAM-RAM sections 5.2/5.3/8/11 and the existing burst
implementation's byte-address/alignment contract. RAM capacity remains
134,217,728 bytes; the unchanged 16-bit BRAM address field reaches the lower
65,536 bytes. Full endpoint sums remain 33 and 17 bits. This is an exact
logic optimization, not a narrower address field or changed acceptance rule.

For unsigned x, x > 2^n iff a higher bit is set, or bit n and at least one
lower bit are set. Applying that identity to the existing endpoint sums
removes the second carry comparison. Equality at the memory boundary remains
accepted. Overflow, zero length, invalid words and alignment are still rejected.
Descriptor fields, status, payloads, service clocks and all reset/stall behavior
remain unchanged. N=64, K=2/4/8, int16 coefficients, headroom, phase precision,
RNE and saturation are not modified.

tools/check_hdl_dma_bounds.py extracts the actual validation expressions and
proves them against independent 64-bit unsigned byte arithmetic for all 64
descriptor input bits, unconstrained. The reference's largest possible sum
is 2^32-1 + 255*16, so its arithmetic cannot wrap. Yosys SAT proves equivalence.
Two mutation tests must fail: removing the overflow bit and rejecting the
exact BRAM endpoint. The proof is combinational descriptor validity only;
transport state, real payloads, timing and integration have separate gates.

The original five-component regression passes 85 tests, including the three
formal checks. After explicit owner-state holds, 60 owner/stream/window tests
pass. After the final ingress factoring, all 27 window tests pass again.
The final tree is covered by 25 unchanged core/queue/formal tests, 33 tests
from the owner/stream follow-up, and the latest 27 window tests (85 total).
This is scoped rerun coverage, not a new single 85-test final-tree run.
Earlier captured arithmetic/reference vectors and
their thresholds are unchanged. No complete root/Python suite was rerun.

The window follow-up removes a redundant outstanding==0 readiness predicate.
Reset clears busy/count, admission requires busy, and closure requires next
count zero; therefore idle implies no outstanding work. That invariant is
now asserted in simulation. Removing its duplicate comparison from start's
diagnostic-counter fanout changes neither legal handshake timing nor counter
widths. Every original deadline, reject, queue, payload, reset and audio probe
is rerun. Three intermediate post-bounds window route sets are retained.

The owner state case now spells out each state's constant hold value rather
than inferring a common hold enable. Its seven state values and three-bit
storage are unchanged. Direct SAT checks every binary combination of encoded
state, bank, status and event inputs against the original next-state function.
Every non-comment token outside that case block is identical, including reset
and register declarations. The embedded proof module is reproducible with
Yosys prep and sat -verify -prove same 1. Supporting module-level equiv_simple
and equiv_induct runs prove all 427 equivalence points for K2/K4/K8, after
async2sync. Those supporting runs assume synchronized asynchronous-reset
behavior and use weak induction; the unconstrained changed-cone proof and
token identity are the primary evidence for this local rewrite.

The top-level ingress factors duplicate lifecycle and cancellation gates.
Active inner sealing/expiry already implies seal_pending or final_cycle;
owner readiness already excludes matching cancellation. Explicit ingress-drop
logic is algebraically its public valid/ready/drop handshake. Simulation
asserts both identities across the same deadline/reset/seal/cancel probes.
No register widths, added cycles, payloads or counters change in this rewrite.

Current source-matched synthesis and routing are regenerated for burst, queue,
owner, stream and full windowed audio. Even the stream netlists changed after
reading the unused burst module; those initial placements were rerun, then
repeated for the owner-state change. Every current placement uses its actual
source/netlist hash. No old placement is reused as current acceptance.
All placements use ECP5-25K speed6 CSFBGA285 at 100MHz, seeds1/2/3, with no
timing-allow-fail. Failed results are retained and never counted as acceptance.

The scope excludes LPDDR3 adapter/PHY, board I/O/global clocks, mapped/device
equivalence, full voice/operator generation, whole-device memory packing and
worst-case workload admission. The 40,000-clock DMA window and 133,333-clock
audio budget are unchanged. Mode A storage and K2 tier cost remain unresolved.

A separate candidate removes the owner expiry cone's cross-bank hit term for
non-ready banks, using the existing unique-live-tag invariant. Direct SAT
proves transition equivalence under that invariant; another all-input proof
establishes preservation by the actual admission rule and both bank next-state
functions. Reset with both states FREE establishes its base case. The isolated
K4/seed1 combined placement still fails at 92.72137MHz. That candidate remains
outside production, has no regression/three-seed acceptance claim, and is
included only as evidence guiding the next timing investigation.

Independent proof module:
module dma_bounds_formal(input [63:0] descriptor, output equivalent);
    wire [32:0] ram_end = {1'b0, descriptor[63:32]} + {21'd0, descriptor[15:8], 4'd0};
    wire [16:0] bram_end = {1'b0, descriptor[31:16]} + {5'd0, descriptor[15:8], 4'd0};
    // Preserve full endpoint widths, including address overflow. For a 2^n
    // limit, an endpoint is above it when a higher bit is set, or bit n and
    // any lower bit are both set. Exact endpoints remain legal. This avoids
    // a second wide carry comparison after the address sum.
    wire bad_descriptor = !descriptor[0] || descriptor[15:8] == 0
                       || descriptor[35:32] != 0 || descriptor[19:16] != 0
                       || (|ram_end[32:28]) || (ram_end[27] && (|ram_end[26:0]))
                       || (bram_end[16] && (|bram_end[15:0]));

    // Independent reference uses 64-bit unsigned byte arithmetic. Its largest
    // endpoint is 2^32-1 + 255*16, which is representable without overflow.
    wire [63:0] reference_ram_end = {32'd0, descriptor[63:32]} + {52'd0, descriptor[15:8], 4'd0};
    wire [63:0] reference_bram_end = {48'd0, descriptor[31:16]} + {52'd0, descriptor[15:8], 4'd0};
    wire reference_bad = !descriptor[0] || descriptor[15:8] == 0
                      || descriptor[63:32] % 16 != 0 || descriptor[31:16] % 16 != 0
                      || reference_ram_end > 64'd134217728 || reference_bram_end > 64'd65536;
    assign equivalent = bad_descriptor == reference_bad;
endmodule
